DPWH</td><td>Planning Service to:a. develop a strategic and well-coordinated plan aligned with disaster risk reduction and infrastructure resilience, to ensure the efficient utilization of allotments and prevent the accumulation of unobligated funds; andb. coordinate closely with the Accounting Office to establish a monitoring and evaluation system to track the performance of funded projects, enabling the identification of areas for improvement.</td></tr><tr><td>5. LWUA</td><td>All concerned personnel to:a.Marawi Cityi. conduct a re-evaluation of the actual project status based on approved POW and submit project milestone reports in accordance with LWUA Office Order No. 154.19; andii. submit the required utilization reports pursuant to Section 4.6 of COA Circular 94-013 and, if warranted, initiate the process for the return of the unutilized fund balance amounting to ₱4.55 million.b.Cagayan de Oro Cityi. set timeline for the revision of the MOA template to incorporate an explicit provision specifying the project duration in all future agreements between LWUA and Water Districts for DRRM-funded projects; andc. strengthen LWUA's oversight role in monitoring and supervising the construction and rehabilitation of water supply systems to ensure compliance with project timelines, quality standards, and funding requirements.</td></tr><tr><td>6. NHA</td><td>To minimize the delays in project implementation and to ensure the immediate completion of housing units, we reiterated the previous recommendations that Management:a. ensure full coordination with the concerned government agencies, particularly the LGUs and DENR to address and resolve the issues/problems contributing to the delayed construction/completion, and to promote effective and more synchronized planning and implementation of projects;b. closely monitor timelines for completion of projects by the contractors and address the causes of the delays/suspensions and grant of time extensions;c. act immediately on terminated contracts with issued notice and order of termination; andd. for projects with LGUs as implementing agencies, coordinate and strictly monitor the project accomplishment for the immediate completion of the housing units.</td></tr><tr><td>7. PCG</td><td>Concerned PCG units to facilitate early procurement activities to avoid delays and ensure that the allotted funds will be fully utilized for their intended purposes.</td></tr></table>

## Absence of a comprehensive DRRM Plan

64. The absence of a comprehensive DRRM Plan runs counter to the intent and requirements outlined in RA No. 10121, thus increasing exposure to operational and service interruptions, infrastructure damage, and the absence of funds for swift and effective emergency response. (NPC and PNRI)

64.1 Section 22(e) of RA No. 10121 requires that all departments, bureaus, offices, and agencies of the government are authorized to use a portion of their appropriations to implement projects designed to address DRRM activities.  
64.2 In NPC, the Team noted the absence of a comprehensive DRRM Plan, which runs counter to said RA. It may expose the agency to operational interruptions, service disruptions, infrastructure damage, and financial losses.  
64.3 Likewise, the PNRI lacks specific funds for prepositioned and emergency response logistics necessary for a swift and more effective response. This funding gap hinders PNRI’s ability to fully support its key role in radiological and nuclear emergency preparedness, as mandated by RA No. 10121.  
64.4 The Audit Teams recommended and Management agreed to require the concerned units to expedite the preparation and finalization of the DRRM Plan and Budget and ensure its implementation in the succeeding years.

## Non-termination of contract

65. The agency has not yet taken steps to either terminate the contract totaling ₱29.44 million or assume responsibility for the Marawi Regional Center

## Project, despite the contractor/project already experiencing a negative slippage of over 15 percent, contrary to GPPB Resolution No. 05-2019. (PTNI)

65.1. GPPB Resolution No. 05-2019 provides guidance on contract termination due to 15 percent negative slippage by the contractor in infrastructure projects. The said resolution requires that the implementing unit shall initiate termination of the contract or takeover of the work by the administration or contract. It shall also take proper transitory measures to minimize work disruptions, e.g., takeover by the administration while negotiations or rebidding are ongoing.  
65.2. The Team noted that the agency has not yet taken steps to either terminate the contract or assume responsibility for the Marawi Regional Center Project, which was awarded to Wismon Construction and Development Corporation for ₱29.44 million, despite the project experiencing a negative slippage of over 15 percent  
65.3. Such inaction is inconsistent with the said guidelines and may result in a delay in realizing the anticipated benefits of the project for the public.  
65.4. We recommended and Management agreed to initiate the termination of the subject contract or take-over of work in accordance with the guidelines.

## Other significant observations relative to DRRM-related PAPs

66. The construction of new five-slope protection projects with a total contract cost of ₱1.01 billion charged against the NDRRMF is contrary to NDRRMC Memorandum Circular No. 110 s. 2021. Moreover, their failure to submit the required documents for funding raises concerns about their compliance with the procedural requirements. (DPWH IV-B)

66.1. Section 5.1.8.4 of the NDRRMC Memorandum Circular No. 110 s. 2021 provides that the NDRRMF cannot be used to cover new construction of auxiliary parts of roads and bridges, such as slope protection and drainage structures, as these should be funded from agency or LGUs’ developmental budgets.  
66.2. The Audit Team noted that the Regional Office's use of the NDRRMF for the construction of new five-slope protection projects with a total contract cost of ₱1,009,137,037.86 is contrary to said Memorandum Circular. Moreover, their failure to submit the required documents for funding, as mandated under the same Memorandum Circular, raises concerns about their compliance with the procedural requirements.

66.3. The foregoing not only casts doubt on the appropriateness of charging these projects against the NDRRMF but also suggests a potential misallocation of resources, which could impede the government's ability to prioritize projects and effectively respond to and recover from disasters.  
66.4. We recommended that the Management submit immediately the written justification explaining why the Regional Office proceeded with the procurement and implementation of the five-slope protection projects, despite the explicit prohibition on funding such projects with the NDRRMF, and henceforth, ensure that all projects proposed for NDRRMF allocation undergo thorough selection to verify their eligibility according to the prescribed guidelines.

67. Unnecessary/excessive expenditures totaling to at least ₱1.64 million for various meetings and the absence of proper budget monitoring violated PD No. 1445 and pertinent rules and regulations. (DEPED)

67.1. Section 2 of PD No. 1445 provides that all resources of the government shall be managed, expended or utilized in accordance with laws, and regulations, and safeguarded against loss or wastage through illegal or improper disposition, with a view to ensuring efficiency, economy and effectiveness in the operations of government. The responsibility to take care that such policy is faithfully adhered to rests directly with the chief or head of the government agency concerned.  
67.2. The Team observed the following observations for DRRM-related expenditures:

a. unnecessary disbursements of ₱266,166.51 on meals/snacks for various meetings conducted  
b. excessive and extravagant disbursement of at least ₱1,258,035.00 for rentals of venue, board and lodging, and meals  
c. unnecessary reimbursements of ₱118,673.20 for traveling expenses despite having been included in the total cost of the events  
d. excessive gasoline consumption and incomplete data on trip tickets and sales invoices (no amount indicated)

67.3. In addition, discrepancy amounting to ₱2,175,667.03 were observed between the reported Disaster Preparedness and Response Program (DPRP) Fund of the Statement of Appropriations, Allotments, Obligations, Disbursements and Balances (SAAODB) and the report submitted to the NDRRMC, as shown below:

<table><tr><td rowspan="2">Particulars</td><td colspan="2">Reported NDRRMF for CY 2024</td><td rowspan="2">Variance</td></tr><tr><td>Per NDRRMC Report</td><td>Per SAAODB Report</td></tr><tr><td>QRF</td><td>₱ 3,000,000,000.00</td><td>₱ 3,000,000,000.00</td><td>₱ -</td></tr><tr><td>DPRP</td><td>2,167,571,667.03</td><td>2,165,396,000.00</td><td>2,175,667.03</td></tr><tr><td>Total</td><td>₱ 5,167,571,667.03</td><td>₱ 5,165,396,000.00</td><td>₱ 2,175,667.03</td></tr></table>

67.4. Furthermore, the non-submission of the accomplishment report on the implementation and utilization of the DPRP Funds and QRF was also noted.

67.5. All these observations contravene the existing laws, rules, and regulations in ensuring that the public funds are used effectively and efficiently to avoid possible loss and wastage.

67.6. We recommended that Management:

a. submit justifications/basis and supporting documents for the noted unnecessary/excessive disbursements and henceforth, strictly comply with the rules and regulations on government expenditures;  
b. reconcile the noted discrepancy and submit a revised report to the Audit Team; and  
c. submit the accomplishment and utilization reports as of December 31, 2024, and every end of the year.

68. The disbursement voucher for the procured relief goods of ₱3 million intended for the victims of Typhoon Kristine was processed and paid despite the incomplete documentary requirements prescribed in COA Circular No. 2012- 001. Additionally, other expenses incurred during the relief operations, totaling ₱0.18 million, were reimbursed contrary to COA Circular No. 97-002. (PEZA)

68.1. COA Circular 2012-01 prescribes the guidelines and documentary requirements for common government transactions. While COA Circular No. 97-002 prescribes that cash should be handled under the general principles of the imprest system, such that all payments must be made by check.  
68.2. The Audit Team noted that the agency still processed and paid ₱3 million for the supply and delivery of various relief goods without the complete documentary requirements supporting the claims as specified under COA Circular No. 2012-001. Also, copies of the contract and relevant procurement documents were not submitted to the Audit Team within five working days from their issuance.  
68.3. On the other hand, it also reimbursed expenses totaling ₱179,449.00 contrary to COA Circular No. 97-002 due to inadequate compliance with the procurement and disbursement regulations.

68.4. This indicate lapses in internal control that may result in increased risks due to fraud or mismanagement of funds.  
68.5. We recommended that Management:

a. submit the lacking documents, and henceforth, strictly adhere to the COA Circular 2012-001;  
b. submit an explanation why expenses totaling ₱179,449.00 were reimbursed and were not paid by issuing checks, and henceforth, refrain from paying expenses through reimbursement basis unless absolutely necessary; and  
c. ensure that copies of contracts and relevant procurement documents are submitted to the Audit Team within the prescribed timeline.

69. The inclusion of service vehicle rental of ₱2.19 million as pay item in the Programs of Work (POW) of infrastructure projects enables the agency to acquire vehicles without the necessary authority under DBM BC No. 2022-1, and without undergoing procurement as required under RA No. 9184, consequently undermining the prudent and judicious acquisition, utilization, and rental of government motor vehicles. (NHA)

69.1. Competitive bidding is the primary method of procurement under RA No. 9184 to ensure that the government gets the best value for its money. While DBM BC No. 2022-1 provides the guidelines on the acquisition, use, rental and replacement of government motor vehicles.  
69.2. The practice by NHA to acquire vehicles by including it in the POW of infrastructure projects despite the absence of the required approval under DBM BC No. 2022-1, and without undergoing competitive procurement as required under RA No. 9184, failed to ensure the prudent and judicious acquisition, use, and rental of government vehicles.  
69.3. We recommended that Management ensure that all rentals/acquisition of vehicles strictly comply with the procurement law and relevant DBM rules and regulations.

## B. Financial Assistance, Cash and In-Kind Donations

Cash donations that are unutilized/underutilized or not utilized according to the intended purpose  
70. Unutilized donations of ₱3.92 million intended for the relief operations for the victims of Typhoons Yolanda and Odette were not remitted to the BTr, as required under COA Circular No. 2014-002; instead, ₱2.87 million was utilized for the relief operations for the victims of Typhoon Kristine, despite the absence of any condition or an agreement with the donor specifying its reallocation. (PEZA)

70.1. Section IV (B.13) of Circular No. 2014-002 dated April 15, 2014, provides that all unutilized cash donations after the purpose has been served shall be remitted to the BTr, if no condition to return any unused amount to the donor. It shall be returned to the donor if specified in the agreement.  
70.2. The Team noted that the unutilized donations of ₱3.92 million were not returned or remitted to the BTr because the Management decided to use a portion of the funds totaling ₱2.87 million for relief operations for Typhoon Kristine, even without proper authority from the donors.  
70.3. The unauthorized use of donated funds violated the said Circular and undermined the donors’ intended purposes.  
70.4. We recommended that Management submit any agreement or authorization from the donors, if any, confirming the use of the donated funds for other purposes; otherwise, require the concerned officials to justify the unauthorized fund utilization.  
70.5. Further, we recommended that Management remit the unutilized cash donations to the BTr.

Lapses in the accounting and proper monitoring of cash and in-kind donations

71. Unutilized cash donations of ₱3.58 million were not recorded in the books of accounts resulting in an understatement of Cash and Trust Liabilities accounts nor remitted to the BTr. (PEZA)

71.1. Fair presentation of the financial statements requires the faithful representation of the effects of transactions, other events, and conditions in accordance with the definitions and recognition criteria for assets, liabilities, income and expenses.

71.2. The Audit Team noted that of the total unutilized cash balance of ₱6.18 million as of year-end, only ₱2.60 million was recorded in the books of accounts, resulting in the understatement of Cash and Trust Liabilities accounts by ₱3.58 million as of December 31, 2024, due to the incomplete recognition of cash donations. In addition, said amount was also not remitted to the BTr, as required.  
71.3. We recommended that Management record in the books of accounts the unutilized cash donations of ₱3.58 million and remit to the BTr as required by existing regulations.

## C. Fund Transfers

## Unliquidated fund transfers to Implementing Agencies

72. Subsidy funds amounting to ₱1.21 billion released to 87 Electric Cooperatives (ECs) from CYs 2019 to 2024 recorded under the Due from NGOs/POs account remained unliquidated for over 1 to 15 years, in violation of the MOA entered into by and between NEA and ECs. (NEA)

72.1. NEA received subsidy funds from the NG and distributed them to different ECs for electrification projects such as the Sitio Electrification Program (SEP), Barangay Line Enhancement Program (BLEP), Housewiring Program, and other calamity grants caused by typhoons, earthquakes, and armed conflicts.  
72.2. According to the MOA between NEA and ECs, the ECs have a six-month timeframe to implement and complete the projects after receiving subsidy funds from NEA. If there is a concern about completing the projects within the allowed timeframe, ECs must submit a written extension request to NEA, with a maximum extension of three months. Additionally, ECs must settle/liquidate the subsidy funds within three months from project completion.  
72.3. Further, the NEA has the authority to take necessary measures/actions if ECs do not adhere strictly to the MOA provisions. In addition, the NEA should conduct periodic inspections to monitor project progress and fund utilization and recommend remedial/corrective actions for any issues/ problems that may arise.  
72.4. As of December 31, 2024, ₱1.21 billion released to ECs has been outstanding for over 1 to 15 years, representing subsidy funds to 87 ECs. This substantial unliquidated balance, which has not been completed and evaluated/inspected within the specified timeline, indicates that NEA did

not effectively enforce the liquidation or settlement of the subsidy funds by the ECs.

72.5. This resulted in the accumulation of unliquidated subsidy funds, increasing the risks of inefficient fund utilization and project delays.  
72.6. We recommended that Management require the:

a. Accounts Servicing Division (ASD) to:

ensure the timely evaluation of the liquidation documents submitted by the ECs totaling ₱336.92 million by setting a clear review timeline and promptly recommend the recording of the final subsidy liquidation of ₱72.75 million;  
demand the submission of liquidation documents amounting to ₱156.43 million and the return of ₱35.80 million unutilized subsidy funds from the ECs; and  
• reconcile the excess return of ₱0.032 million and refund the amount to the concerned EC, if necessary.

b. DRRM Department (DRRMD) and Total Electrification and Renewable Development Department (TEREDD) to:

strictly monitor the ongoing projects worth ₱353.05 million for compliance with approved plans, recommending remedial actions to avoid delays and ensure timely completion and fund liquidation;  
• expedite the inspection of completed projects amounting to ₱252.24 million by setting clear inspection timelines for prompt closure;  
address manpower deficiencies through a comprehensive workload assessment and allocation of sufficient personnel to meet operational and internal control requirements effectively;  
strictly enforce the penal provisions of the MOA and pursue appropriate legal actions against non-compliant ECs, issue demand letters and implement strict monitoring to expedite liquidation of funds; and  
• establish clear deadlines, require periodic status reports, and impose sanctions or withhold future fund releases for noncompliant ECs and coordinate with relevant oversight agencies

to expedite the settlement of long-outstanding accounts, particularly those unliquidated for over 15 years.

73. Calamity-funded projects totaling ₱147.88 million implemented by nine ECs were not effectively executed as planned and had several deficiencies, including: a) failure to comply with RA No. 9184 and its RIRR; b) actual date of project implementation was not aligned with the timeline outlined in the MOA; c) completion of the projects was delayed ranging from 66 days to 252 days; d) energization of the projects was delayed ranging from 66 days to 252 days; and e) inspection of the completed projects was delayed ranging from 45 days to 972 days, in violation of MOA between NEA and the ECs, resulting in delays in delivering essential services to the intended beneficiaries. (NEA)

## 73.1. The Audit Team noted the following:

a. Weak enforcement of the provisions of the MOA

The provisions of the MOA were not strictly enforced by NEA to the ECbeneficiaries, particularly Item Nos. 3, 4, 5, and 7. In fact, in its reply to AQM No. 2024-001 (2024), dated September 19, 2024, the Technical Service informed that, due to the urgency of the immediate restoration of power and rehabilitation of damaged distribution lines, the EC may not strictly adhere to RA No. 9184. This indicates that NEA tolerates the practice of the ECs of not strictly complying with the MOA and other relevant rules.

b. Inadequate understanding of the applicable law, rules and policy

While it was argued that reimbursement for calamity fund requests exempts the EC from strict adherence to RA No. 9184 and its RIRR, NEA records confirm that the MOA explicitly mandates such compliance, a requirement further reinforced by various NEA memoranda. Reimbursement serves solely as a payment mechanism for actual incurred expenses under the established rules and does not provide authority to deviate from the MOA or applicable regulations.

c. DRMMD’s laxity in monitoring and supervising the project

There are no records or reports to document regular meetings or monitoring activities. DRMMD's submissions indicate that project inspections occurred only after energization, and the division lacked essential project management tools, such as task comments, progress tracking, and performance dashboards, which are vital for effective feedback loops.

d. Absence of established guidelines in the conduct of inspection of projects.

NEA has no existing policy guidelines relative to the conduct of inspection of completed projects, except for NEA Memorandum No. 2022-35 dated June 15, 2022, which simply states that periodic inspection shall be conducted by the NEA for purposes stated in the said memorandum.

e. Absence of a feedback mechanism

DRMMD has not effectively established channels that allow for both giving and receiving feedback in a timely manner. A management representative explained that due to manpower shortages and budget constraints, project inspections are typically conducted only after project energization rather than during earlier stages.

f. NEA failed to enforce the penal provisions embodied in the MOA

The noted deficiencies are common to most ECs. Despite that, NEA failed to take appropriate action to amend the incorrect practice of the ECs.

73.2. The delays in project implementation, completion, and inspection have deferred both the liquidation and the closing of the subsidy fund in the EC’s records. Furthermore, these setbacks in electrification projects compromise timely access to essential services for beneficiaries. Prompt and effective project execution is, therefore, crucial for the well-being and development of the intended recipients.

73.3. We recommended that Management require the DRRM Division to:

a. conduct regular progress reviews, implement periodic inspections with validated reports, and enforce the liquidation of subsidy funds within the approved timeline to prevent delays and ensure full compliance with project implementation schedules;  
b. establish clear and efficient communication channels for timely feedback exchange, including regular meetings, digital platforms, and tracking systems;  
c. integrate advanced technologies, such as task tracking systems and remote monitoring tools, and address manpower constraints through strategic staffing or outsourcing to optimize inspections and ensure timely project evaluations prior to energization;

d. craft a policy guideline for inspecting completed projects to ensure that inspections are conducted within the specified timeline, to avoid delays in the liquidation of the subsidy funds received; and  
e. review and revise the MOA provisions to align with actual NEA and EC practices, and strictly enforce key MOA provisions specificall Items 3, 4, 5, and 7 to ensure ECs’ compliance and prevent delays in project implementation, completion, energization, and inspection.

74. The Marawi Recovery, Rehabilitation and Reconstruction Program (MRRRP) and 2020 GAA-Philippine Counterpart on the JICA Donation for BARMM ECs subsidy fund amounting to ₱424.08 million for the implementation of various projects of Lanao Del Sur Electric Cooperative (LASURECO) were not efficiently and effectively executed in accordance with the approved plans. (NEA)

74.1. The Team noted the following lapses, such as:

DRRMD’s inadequate evaluation of the project prior to the release of the subsidy fund to the EC, lax monitoring and supervision during the implementation, and failure to recommend appropriate remedial actions within a reasonable time to address the identified issues in the project implementation resulted in delays in the completion/energization and inspection of the project as well as the issuance of Certificate of Final Inspection and Acceptance (CFIA);  
ASD’s lack of sufficient action to enforce the liquidation of the completed projects and the return of unutilized funds within the allowable timeline, as provided in the MOA, resulted in an unreasonable delay in the liquidation of subsidy funds. In addition, this also caused a delay in the release of the additional subsidy funds for other projects;  
Financial Services Department’s (FSD) inadequate review of the MOA led to incorrect computation of the EAO, depriving the EC of the additional subsidy fund that should have been used for the project;  
DRRMD and ASD have been operating without a reasonable manpower complement to fully satisfy the scope/complexities of the operating requirements and further strengthening its internal control; and

Non-enforcement of the penal provisions embodied in Item No. 6 of the MOA. The provisions of the MOA were not strictly enforced by NEA to the EC-beneficiaries, particularly Item Nos. 3, 4, 5, and 7. In fact, in its reply to AQM No. 2024-001 (2024), dated September 19, 2024, the Technical Service informed that, due to the urgency of the immediate restoration of power and rehabilitation of damaged distribution lines, the EC may not strictly adhere to RA No. 9184. This indicates that NEA tolerates the practice of the ECs not strictly complying with the MOA and other relevant rules.

74.2. As a result of lax monitoring, all payments made using unutilized subsidy funds that were realigned without the proper approval of NEA are deemed invalid. Further, the use of subsidy fund for purposes other than those stated in the MOA constitutes an unlawful diversion of funds and is deemed misappropriation of government funds.

74.3. The unreasonable delays in project completion, energization, and inspection have led to setbacks in issuing the CFIA, project closure, and timely liquidation of subsidy funds. These delays may result in increased project costs or reduced scope to cover additional financial requirements. Late inspections risk discrepancies, as the original condition of the project may be altered due to repairs or modifications. Furthermore, delayed liquidation and failure to return unutilized funds hinder the release of additional subsidies for ongoing and future projects, increase the risk of lost supporting documents, and expose unremitted funds to potential misuse, loss, or fraud.

74.4. We recommended that Management require the:

## a. DRRM Division to:

review the unauthorized and unsupported realignment of subsidy fund made by LASURECO and recommend appropriate action to rectify the error committed and pursue other legal actions as necessary to address any violation committed by the EC and its personnel or officials;  
require LASURECO to strictly comply with the requirements of Item No. D, IV of NEA Memorandum No. 2019-001, which mandates securing NEA’s approval before implementing any realignment of unutilized/excess funds;  
evaluate ECs’ budget proposals to ensure accuracy, prevent irregularities, and verify alignment with approved project areas. Review and revise LASURECO’s project plans to support timely completion, establish clear and approved

timelines for remaining works, and conduct regular coordination meetings to address implementation delays and enforce corrective actions;

coordinate with NHA to determine the status of unconstructed shelters affecting project completion and take appropriate action on the NHA’s response;  
expedite inspection of completed but uninspected projects, and validate the purchase of materials for operations and computerization, ensuring submission of After-Mission Reports with supporting documents;  
assess the necessity of constructing the Perimeter Fence, Corporate, Engineering, and Warehouse Building project, and if deemed unnecessary, facilitate the return of the unused funds to the National Treasury; and  
strictly monitor and supervise the ongoing and unimplemented projects to ensure LASURECO’s compliance with the MOA, NEA-issued memorandum, COA-issuances and other relevant law, rules and regulations;

## b. ASD to:

review thoroughly the MOA prior to approval by the representatives of both NEA and the EC to ensure that all added provisions are in accordance with applicable laws, rules and regulations; and  
demand the immediate liquidation of the completed projects, and the return of the unutilized subsidy fund amounting to ₱2.65 million from LASURECO. Otherwise, recommend appropriate legal actions to NEA Management for LASURECO’s non-compliance.

## D. Procurement

## Noncompliance with RA No. 9184 and its RIRR

75. The procedures undertaken in the procurement of the supply and delivery of various relief goods worth ₱3 million for the victims of Typhoon Kristine through Negotiated Procurement-Emergency Cases were not in compliance with the RIRR of RA No. 9184. (PEZA)

75.1. Annex “H” of RIRR of RA No. 9184 provides the specific guidelines and procedures to be undertaken through Negotiated Procurement-Emergency Cases.  
75.2. The agency’s procurement of relief goods worth ₱3 million through Negotiated Procurement-Emergency Cases did not comply with Sections IV and V, Annex “H” of the RIRR of RA No. 9184 due to inadequate documentation, absence of supplier eligibility confirmation, and improper reference to brand names.  
75.3. These deficiencies may have compromised the transparency, competitiveness, and fairness of the procurement process, increasing the risk of awarding contracts to unqualified suppliers.  
75.4. We recommended that Management submit the lacking documents to the Audit Team, and henceforth, strictly adhere to the procedural requirements of Annex “H” of the RIRR.

## E. Accounting and Submission of required DRRMF reports

## Failure to submit DRRMF reports and inadequate disclosure in the Notes to FS

## 76. Non-submission of the Report on the Receipt and utilization of the DRRMF and inadequate disclosure contrary to COA Circular No. 2014-002 dated April 15, 2014. (DICT, DPWH, PCUP, PHIVOLCS, PEZA, PTNI)

76.1. Section V of COA Circular No. 2014-002 dated April 15, 2014 provides that government agencies with DRRMF allocation from the GAA and/or cash donations received from local and/or foreign sources shall prepare and submit to NDRRMC through the OCD, on the 5th day following the end of each month, the Report on the Receipt and Utilization of the DRRMF sourced from GAA and Report on the Receipt and Utilization of Cash Donations. The reports shall be furnished to the respective COA Auditor. Furthermore, these reports shall be posted on the official websites of the implementing agency, OCD, and NDRRMC.

76.2. Likewise, Section VII of said Circular provides that the information on DRRM, not evident on the face of the FS, shall be disclosed in the Notes to FS, such as, but not limited to inventories (total procurement, issuance and balance of inventory procured from GAA and cash donations, quantity received, issued and balance of donated relief goods per category), PPE (procurement out of GAA and Cash donations, receipt of donations, disposal/transfer), and Trust Liabilities-DRRMF.

76.3. The DICT Visayas Cluster 2 acknowledges the non-submission of the prescribed DRRM reports to the oversight agencies, inadequate disclosures in the Notes to FS, and non-posting of the reports on the agency website. However, the Office submitted the reports to DICT Central Office for consolidation. In its action plan, it directed the DRRM Focal person to coordinate with the Finance Office to ensure compliance with the monthly reporting of DRRM-related expenses, which are to be submitted to OCD and COA.  
76.4. The DPWH, PHIVOLCS, and PTNI did not comply with the rules for accounting and reporting for the DRRMF reports and posting the same on their websites because they are unaware of the requirement to prepare and submit a report about DRRM.  
76.5. The PCUP failed to submit to the Audit Team the required detailed accomplishment reports and supporting documents for the implementation of PAPs intended for DRRM and Climate Change Adaptation and Mitigation (CCAM). Although the CY 2024 Accomplishment Report of the agency included a brief narrative mentioning activities, the report lacked sufficient detail, and supporting documents were not attached.  
76.6. In PEZA, the utilization of cash donations, which were originally not intended for Typhoon Kristine, was not adequately accounted for and reported using the Registry of Cash Donations and Utilization as required under the said COA Circular.  
76.7. Non-preparation of the DRRM reports and adequate disclosure in the Notes to FS and on the agency website can lead to a lack of transparency and accountability in fund allocation and utilization. Without these reports, agencies may struggle to monitor and assess the use of funds, which could lead to inefficiencies or the misallocation of resources. These reports are crucial for building trust in financial management. Its absence could reduce transparency, undermine public confidence, and make it harder to assess fund use properly.  
76.8. The Audit Teams recommended that Management ensure that the required reports are prepared and submitted to the oversight agencies within the required timelines and posted on their websites.  
76.9. Further, the Audit Teams recommended that Management disclose the required information regarding the DRRM in the Notes to FS.

## F. Allocation of LDRRMF

## Insufficient Allocation for LDRRMF

77. Allocation of not less than five percent of the estimated revenue from regular sources for LDRRMF as required under Section 21 of RA No. 10121 was not met by 25 LGUs.

77.1. Section 21 of RA No. 10121 provides that the LDRRMF amounting to not less than five percent of the estimated revenue from regular sources shall be set aside to support disaster risk management activities such as, but not limited to the pre-disaster preparedness programs including training, purchase of disaster response and rescue equipment, supplies and medicines, for post-disaster activities, and payment of premiums on calamity insurance.  
77.2. However, the audit noted that the funds of 25 LGUs were not able to meet the required allocation under Section 21 of RA No. 10121. Details are presented in Annex D.  
77.3. Insufficient allocation of LDRRMF may hinder the LGU’s objectives in mitigating the impact of disasters by a fully-equipped, well-trained response teams, and available infrastructures.  
77.4. The Audit Teams recommended that Management strictly observe the allocation of five percent of the estimated revenue from regular sources for LDRRM to support disaster risk management activities pursuant to Section 21 of RA No. 10121.

## G. Utilization of LDRRMF

## Low Utilization of LDRRMF

78. Seventy LGUs did not optimally utilize the allotment under the LDRRMF, with a low utilization rate ranging from 1.41 to 67.16 percent, contrary to the purposes and intents of the RA No. 10121; thus, defeating the objectives of strengthening the capacity to build disaster-resilient communities and to enhance disaster preparedness at all levels.

78.1. Section 21 of RA No. 10121 provides that the LDRRMF shall cover the 30 percent lump-sum allocation for QRF and the 70 percent allocation for disaster prevention and mitigation, preparedness, response, rehabilitation and recovery.

78.2. However, it was noted that 70 LGUs were not able to optimally utilize their LDRRMF, with a low utilization rate ranging from 1.41 to 67.16 percent. A summary of LGUs’ utilization rate is presented in Table 14, while the details are presented in Annex E.

Table 14: LGUs with Low Utilization Rate of LDRRMF

<table><tr><td rowspan="2">Region</td><td colspan="2">LDRRMF</td></tr><tr><td>No. of LGUs*</td><td>Utilization Rate (%)</td></tr><tr><td>NCR</td><td>4</td><td>10.08 to 52</td></tr><tr><td>CAR</td><td>2</td><td>33.26 to 48</td></tr><tr><td>I</td><td>2</td><td>1.41 to 7.37</td></tr><tr><td>II</td><td>3</td><td>9.95 to 50</td></tr><tr><td>III</td><td>8</td><td>17.55 to 60.33</td></tr><tr><td>IV-A</td><td>7</td><td>25.19 to 57.71</td></tr><tr><td>IV-B</td><td>3</td><td>17.50 to 39.66</td></tr><tr><td>V</td><td>2</td><td>42.22 to 55.12</td></tr><tr><td>VI</td><td>9</td><td>15.24 to 59.14</td></tr><tr><td>VII</td><td>6</td><td>8.15 to 48.72</td></tr><tr><td>VIII</td><td>6</td><td>16 to 63.38</td></tr><tr><td>IX</td><td>3</td><td>28.48 to 38.23</td></tr><tr><td>X</td><td>3</td><td>16.26 to 41.94</td></tr><tr><td>XI</td><td>7</td><td>46.01 to 67.16</td></tr><tr><td>XII</td><td>4</td><td>60.10</td></tr><tr><td>XIII</td><td>1</td><td>38.81</td></tr><tr><td>Total</td><td>70</td><td>1.41 to 67.16</td></tr></table>

\* Including 20 LGUs with similar observation, but the utilization rate was not specified

78.3. The low utilization of the LDRRMF defeated the objectives of RA No. 10121 on strengthening the capacity to build disaster-resilient communities and to enhance disaster preparedness at all levels.  
78.4. The Audit Teams recommended that Management optimize the utilization of its LDRRMF to lessen the socio-economic and environmental impacts of disasters in the community, including projected climate risks, and to enhance disaster preparedness and response capabilities of the community.

## Improper Charging of Disbursements

79. Funds of 251 LGUs, amounting to at least ₱316.03 million, were utilized for purposes other than those allowed for the implementation of disaster programs and activities, in violation of COA Circular No. 2012-002 and NDRRMC-DBM-DILG Joint Memorandum Circular (JMC) No. 2013-1.

79.1. Section 3 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 dated September 11, 2012 states that the LDRRMF shall be used to support disaster risk management activities such as, but not limited to the following:

3.1 Pre-disaster preparedness programs such as training of personnel, and purchase of life-saving and rescue equipment, and supplies and medicines;  
3.2 Post-disaster activities such as repair and rehabilitation of public infrastructures and purchase of office/school equipment damaged by calamities during the budget year;  
3.3 Payment of insurance premiums on property if indemnity includes damages or loss due to fire, earthquake, storm, or other casualties and on the personnel accident insurance of Accredited Community Disaster Volunteers; and  
3.4 Relief and recovery programs in communities or areas stricken by disasters, calamities, epidemics or complex emergencies.

79.2. Item 5.0 of the NDRRMC-DBM-DILG JMC No. 2013-1 dated March 25, 2013, identifies sample eligible PAPs, to which the LDRRMF can be used.  
79.3. However, it was noted that funds of 251 LGUs, amounting to at least ₱316.03 million, were utilized for purposes other than those allowed for the implementation of disaster programs and activities in violation of the provisions mentioned above.  
79.4. Table 15 summarizes the LGUs per region that charged unrelated disbursements to LDRRMF, including those with similar observations, but the amount disbursed was not specified. Details are presented in Annex F.

Table 15: List of LGUs with Improper Charging of Disbursements

79.5. Charging of expenditures that are not allowed under pertinent regulations adversely affects the availability of the LDRRMF for disaster-related activities.  
79.6. The Audit Teams recommended that Management strictly adhere to the guidelines set forth under COA Circular No. 2012-002 and NDRRMC-DBM-DILG JMC No. 2013-1, and refrain from charging against the LDRRMF those unrelated PAPs.

## Unsupported Disbursements

80. Validity and propriety of disbursements in 69 LGUs could not be ascertained due to the absence or non-submission of complete and/or sufficient supporting documents.

80.1. Various disbursements in 69 LGUs were not duly supported with complete and/or sufficient supporting documents, casting doubt on the validity and propriety of transactions. List of LGUs is presented in Annex G.  
80.2. The Audit Teams recommended that the Management immediately submit the lacking supporting documents for verification, and henceforth, ensure that all disbursements are duly supported with complete and proper documentation before processing claims for payment.

## Deficiencies in the Procurement Process

81. Various deficiencies and/or deviations were noted in the procurement of goods and services by 51 LGUs contrary to the provisions of RA No. 9184 and its RIRR.

81.1. R.A. No. 9184 is promulgated in line with the commitment of the government to promote good governance and its effort to adhere to the principles of transparency, accountability, equity, efficiency and economy in the procurement process.  
81.2. However, audit noted that various deficiencies and/or deviations were noted in the procurement of goods and services by 51 LGUs. A detailed list of LGUs is shown in Annex H.

81.3. Some of the noted deficiencies are as follows:

a. Competitive public bidding was not resorted to in the procurement of various goods;

b. Contracts awarded to suppliers without PhilGEPS registration;  
c. Absence of necessary information and non-submission of the bidding documents;  
d. Request for Quotations was not posted in the PhilGeps Website;  
e. Splitting of contracts; and  
f. Non-imposition of liquidated damages for the delayed deliveries.

81.4. The deficiencies enumerated above defeated the government’s principles of transparency, accountability, equity, efficiency and economy in the procurement process.  
81.5. The Audit Teams recommended that concerned LGUs strictly adhere to R.A 9184 and to other procurement rules and regulations in all their procurement undertakings.

## H. Implementation of PAPs identified in the LDRRMP

## Unimplemented PAPs

82. Various PAPs incorporated in the LDRRMP of 170 LGUs were not implemented, contrary to RA No. 10121; thus, impeding the efficient use of funds for disaster prevention, mitigation, response and rehabilitation to the disadvantage of the public.

82.1. Section 21 of RA No. 10121 provides that LDRRMF shall be used to support disaster risk management activities, such as, but not limited to, the pre-disaster preparedness programs, including training, purchase of disaster response and rescue equipment, supplies, and medicines, for post-disaster activities, and payment of premiums on calamity insurance.  
82.2. The Audit Teams noted that various disaster-related PAPs of 170 LGUs totaling ₱1.68 billion were not implemented. Additionally, 409 PAPs by 46 LGUs were either delayed or unimplemented. The summary of LGUs is presented in Table 16, while the details are presented in Annex I.

Table 16: LGUs with Unimplemented PAPs

<table><tr><td rowspan="3">Region</td><td colspan="4">Unimplemented PAPs based on</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2">Amount Allotted</td><td colspan="2">No. of Unimplemented PAPs</td></tr><tr><td>No. of LGUs*</td><td>Amount</td><td>No. of LGUs</td><td>No. of PAPs</td></tr><tr><td>NCR</td><td>5</td><td>₱ 287,673,665.52</td><td>2</td><td>38</td></tr><tr><td>CAR</td><td>12</td><td>83,941,867.01</td><td>4</td><td>20</td></tr><tr><td>I</td><td>11</td><td>26,412,283.07</td><td>1</td><td>4</td></tr><tr><td>II</td><td>10</td><td>60,531,615.85</td><td>2</td><td>12</td></tr><tr><td>III</td><td>19</td><td>130,545,084.96</td><td>11</td><td>118</td></tr><tr><td>IV-A</td><td>11</td><td>147,519,755.62</td><td>3</td><td>17</td></tr><tr><td>IV-B</td><td>15</td><td>164,055,698.54</td><td>4</td><td>34</td></tr><tr><td>V</td><td>22</td><td>221,966,728.37</td><td>10</td><td>103</td></tr><tr><td>VI</td><td>11</td><td>56,746,530.76</td><td>2</td><td>19</td></tr><tr><td>VII</td><td>5</td><td>11,376,966.40</td><td>-</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>VIII</td><td>15</td><td>92,648,494.30</td><td>5</td><td>34</td></tr><tr><td>IX</td><td>11</td><td>299,292,173.31</td><td>-</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>X</td><td>2</td><td>3,498,374.97</td><td>-</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>XI</td><td>6</td><td>89,304,391.86</td><td>1</td><td>8</td></tr><tr><td>XII</td><td>7</td><td>-</td><td>-</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>XIII</td><td>1</td><td>-</td><td>1</td><td>2</td></tr><tr><td>Total</td><td>170</td><td>₱1,675,513,630.54</td><td>46</td><td>409</td></tr></table>

\* Including 25 LGUs with the same observation, but the amount was not specified.

82.3. Consequently, the aim to strengthen the capacity of the LGUs to build a disaster-resilient community and to enhance disaster preparedness and response at all levels may not be fully achieved.  
82.4. The Audit Teams recommended that Management ensure the timely implementation of all programmed PAPs, in accordance with the respective appropriation ordinance, LDRRMP, and AIP, in order to lessen the socio-economic and environmental impacts of disasters in the community and to enhance disaster preparedness and response capabilities of the LGU and the community.

## Non-Creation and Understaffing of LDRRMO

83. LDRRMOs in 31 LGUs were not fully capacitated/staffed in violation of RA No. 10121; thus, hindering the capacity of the LGUs to deliver efficiently and effectively the necessary services to ensure the safety of their constituents in the event of disaster, calamity, and other fortuitous events.

83.1. Section 12a of RA No. 10121 requires that there shall be established an LDRRMO in every province, city and municipality, and a Barangay Disaster Risk Reduction and Management Committee (BDRRMC) in every barangay, which shall be responsible for setting the direction, development, implementation, and coordination of disaster risk management programs within their territorial jurisdiction.

83.2. However, while there was an LDRRMO created in 31 LGUs, the composition and structure of the office were not fully capacitated to carry out the mandated duties, such as: (a) Administration and Training, (b) Research and Planning, and (c) Operations and Warning. The details are shown in Annex J.  
83.3. Understaffing of the LDRRMOs raised concern about the capacity of the LGUs to deliver efficiently and effectively the necessary services to ensure the safety of their constituents in the event of disaster, calamity, and other fortuitous events.  
83.4. The Audit Teams recommended that the concerned LGUs fill up the mandatory position of each LDRRMO to provide adequate support to DRRM activities.

## Deficiencies in the LDRRMFIP and Local Sanggunian Resolution

84. The utilization of the LDRRMF in 104 LGUs amounting to not less than ₱1.13 billion could not be judiciously evaluated due to the deficiencies in the details of the LDRRMF Investment Plan (LDRRMFIP) and/or the lack of Local Sanggunian resolution declaring a State of Calamity.

84.1. Section 5.1.2 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 provides that LDRRMFIP shall be prepared annually, and it shall present the 30 percent allocation for QRF in a lump sum and the allocation for disaster mitigation, prevention, and preparedness with details as to projects and activities to be funded. The LDRRMFIP shall also include, under a separate caption, the list of projects and activities charged to the unexpended LDRRMF of previous years.  
84.2. Likewise, Section 5.1.3 of COA Circular 2012-002, provides that the release and use of the LDRRMF shall be supported by: a) Local Sanggunian Resolution and the Declaration of State of Calamity for the QRF; and b) the for projects and activities listed in the approved plan as incorporated in the local development plan and annual work plan and financial plan.  
84.3. However, it was noted that the LDRRMFIP of 104 LGUs were not updated or were not responsive to the AIP. Also, various disbursements were charged in the QRF without the approved Local Sanggunian Resolution and the Declaration of State of Calamity. The details are presented in Annex K.  
84.4. The Audit Teams recommended that concerned DRRMOs submit the LDRRMFIP showing the allocation of LDRRMF for both current and the unexpended funds of prior years, and ensure that the use and release of QRF is supported with Local Sanggunian Resolution and the Declaration of State of Calamity.

## I. Donations Received by LGUs

## Unutilized Cash Donations Received

85. Cash donations received by 20 LGUs from other government agencies and other sources totaling at least ₱82.69 million remained unutilized due to the LGUs’ inability to timely identify the appropriate PAPs to be undertaken, contrary to COA Circular No. 2012-002.

85.1. Section 5.2.4 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 provides that funds received from various sources shall be utilized in accordance with the purpose of the grant. In the absence of a specific purpose, the fund shall be used for purposes provided in the LDRRMFIP.  
85.2. However, it was noted that cash donations received by 20 LGUs totaling ₱82.69 million remained unutilized as of year-end, as shown in Table 17.

85.4. The Audit Teams recommended that the Management ensure the full utilization of cash donations for their intended purposes through proper and timely identification, formulation, and implementation of appropriate PAPs.

## J. Prior Years’ Unexpended Balances of the LDRRMF

Unexpended QRF and LDRRMF-MOOE not transferred to Special Trust Fund (STF)  
86. The unexpended balances of the QRF and LDRRMF-MOOE of 163 LGUs, totaling at least ₱1.45 billion, were not transferred to the STF contrary to COA Circular No. 2012-002; thus, it opens an opportunity for the LGU to use it for unrelated DRRM activities.

86.1. Section 5.1.10 of COA Circular No. 2012-12 provides that:

All unexpended/unobligated balance of the QRF and the DRRMF-MOOE shall be transferred to the Special Trust Fund under the account “Trust Liability-DRRM” in the Trust Fund books.

86.2. The Team noted that the unexpended balances of QRF and LDRRMF-MOOE of 163 LGUs, totaling at least ₱1.45 billion, were not transferred to the STF contrary to the aforementioned Circular; thus, it opens an opportunity for the LGU to use it for activities other than those which support DRRM activities.  
86.3. Table 18 summarizes the 163 LGUs with unexpended LDRRMF that have not been transferred to the STF. The details are presented in Annex L.

## Unexpended LDRRMF-Capital Outlay (CO) inappropriately transferred to STF

87. The unexpended/unobligated balance of LDRRMF-CO of 21 LGUs, totaling at least ₱128.98 million, was inappropriately transferred to the STF contrary to COA Circular No. 2012-002.

87.1. Section 5.1.11 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 provides that all unexpended/ unobligated balance of the LDRRMF-CO shall be made continuing in the General Fund (GF) books until the projects are completed. Any savings shall be available for use in the DRRM activities as provided in the LDRRMFIP.  
87.2. However, it was noted that the unexpended balance of LDRRMF-CO of 39 LGUs totaling at least ₱128.98 million was inappropriately transferred to the STF contrary to the above provision.  
87.3. Table 19 summarizes the 21 LGUs with unexpended LDRRMF-CO funds that were inappropriately transferred to the STF. The details are presented in Annex M.

## GF and, henceforth, ensure that all unexpended/unobligated balance of LDRRMF-CO shall be made continuing in the GF books.

## PY unutilized balances of more than five years not reverted to the GF

88. Unutilized balances of LDRRMF under the STF for CY 2018 and below of 60 LGUs, totaling at least ₱367.52 million, were not transferred to the GF contrary to COA Circular No. 2012-002, hindering the provision of funds to support other social services PAPs.

88.1. Section 5.1.13 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 states that any unutilized amount for five years shall be reverted back to the unappropriated surplus of the GF and shall be made available for other social services after subsequent enactment by the Local Sanggunian.  
88.2. However, it was noted that the unutilized balances of LDRRMF under the STF for CY 2018 and below of 60 LGUs totaling at least ₱367.52 million were not reverted back to the GF. The list of the concerned LGUs is summarized in Table 20. The details are presented in Annex N.

88.3. In view thereof, the said amount was not made available for other social services programs of the concerned LGUs.

88.4. The Audit Teams recommended that the Management revert to the GF the unexpended balance of LDRRMF beyond the five-year validity period, to be made available for other social services, in conformity with Section 5.1.13 of COA Circular No. 2012-002.

## PAPs for the PY’s unexpended balances not included in the LDRRMFIP

89. LDRRMFIP of 75 LGUs did not include the PAPs charged to the unexpended balances of LDRRMF under the STF account, in violation of COA Circular No. 2012-002; hence, the validity of the transactions could not be ascertained.

89.1. Section 4.4 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 provides that the LDRRMCs shall decide on the use of the unexpended balance of the LDRRMF, which shall be incorporated in the LDRRMFIP.  
89.2. While, Section 5.1.2 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 provides that the LDRRMFIP shall also include, under a separate caption, the list of PAPs charged to the unexpended LDRRMF of previous years.  
89.3. However, it was noted that the LDRRMFIP of 75 LGUs did not indicate the list of PAPs charged to the unexpended LDRRMF of previous years. As such, the monitoring of the PYs’ LDRRMF utilization could not be undertaken, and the validity of expenditures charged against the fund could not be ascertained. The details are presented in Annex O.  
89.4. The Audit Teams recommended that the Management include in their plans the utilization of the unexpended LDRRMF balances from PYs, and specify the list of PAPs to be charged against the unexpended LDRRMF of PYs in its LDRRMFIP.

## K. Submission of Required Reports

## Unprepared and/or unsubmitted reports

90. A total of 190 LGUs failed to prepare and submit the required: a) Annual LDRRMFIP; b) Registry of Appropriations, Allotments and Obligations (RAAO) for QRF, MOOE and CO; and c) Report on Sources and Utilization of DRRMF, causing difficulty in the evaluation of the LDRRMF utilization.

90.1. Transparency of and accountability in the use of disaster relief aid/donations and NDRRMF have become an issue of considerable importance for donors, bilateral and multilateral organizations, other stakeholders, and the public. This concern is aggravated by the limited information on how these funds are being utilized.  
90.2. As such, various regulations were issued, mandating the preparation and submission of various financial reports and the availability of information

in the receipt of DRRM funds, as well as disaster relief aid/donations in cash and in-kind, viz:

<table><tr><td>Required Report</td><td>Legal Basis</td><td>Frequency of and/or Deadline on Submission</td></tr><tr><td>a) LDRRMFIP</td><td>Section 5.1.2 of COACircular No. 2012-002</td><td>Annually</td></tr><tr><td>b) RAAO for QRF, MOOE and CO</td><td>Section 5.1.4 of COACircular No. 2012-002</td><td>To be maintained and updated regularly</td></tr><tr><td>c) Report on Sources and Utilization of DRRMF</td><td>Section 5.1.5 of COACircular No. 2012-002</td><td>Monthly, on or before 15thday after the end of each month</td></tr></table>

90.3. However, it was noted that 190 LGUs were not able to prepare and/or submit some, if not all, of the afore-cited reports within the prescribed period, precluding the Audit Teams from the timely validation of the receipt and utilization of the LDRRMF. The details are shown in Annex P.  
90.4. The Audit Teams recommended that Management prepare and submit the required financial reports within the prescribed deadline to ensure the availability of information on DRRM funds.

## Accounting and Financial Disclosures

Non-disclosure in the Notes to Financial Statements (FS)

91. Disclosure requirements prescribed in COA Circular No. 2014-002 related to DRRM Funds were not presented in the Notes to FS of 14 LGUs; thus, the information on the utilization and disbursements of the said funds was not adequately disclosed. Moreover, information on the amount and details of the unexpended balance of LDRRMF of 77 LGUs was not adequately disclosed in the Notes to FS as prescribed under COA No. 2012-002.

## Current Year LDRRMF

91.1. COA Circular No. 2014-002 prescribed that information about DRRM not evident on the face of the FS shall be disclosed in the Notes to FS. However, 14 LGUs were unable to disclose the necessary information on the receipt and utilization of DRRM funds; thus, the related information on the utilization and disbursement of this fund was not adequately disclosed. The details are shown in Annex Q.

## Unexpended Balance of PYs’ LDRRMF

91.2. Item 5.1.16 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 states that the amount and details of the unexpended balance of LDRRMF shall be discussed in the Notes to FS.

91.3. However, 77 LGUs failed to provide the necessary disclosures pertinent to the unexpended balance in the Notes to FS. The list of the concerned LGUs are shown in Annex R.  
91.4. The Audit Teams recommended that Management direct the concerned Accountants to fully disclose the receipts of utilization of LDRRMF by providing relevant information in the Notes to FS.

## No separate Subsidiary Ledger (SL) maintained

92. Separate SLs were not maintained by 36 LGUs for transfers of the agency’s unutilized DRRMF to the STF by year of transfer, receipts of NDRRMF by the transferring agency, DRRMF from other LGUs, and other sources by donors, in violation of COA Circular 2012-002.

92.1. Section 5.1.12 of COA Circular 2012-002 provides that a separate SL account under “Trust Liability-DRRM” shall be used to record the transfers of the agency’s unutilized DRRMF to the STF by year of transfer, receipts of NDRRMF by the transferring agency, DRRMF from other LGUs by LGU, and other sources by donor.  
92.2. However, it was noted that 36 LGUs did not maintain a separate SL account as prescribed in the above provision. The non-maintenance of a separate SL account raises doubts about the reliability and propriety of the fund's utilization. The details are presented in Annex S.  
92.3. The Audit Teams recommended that Management direct the concerned Accountants to maintain separate SLs for transfers of the agency’s unutilized DRRMF to the STF by year of transfer, receipts of NDRRMF by the transferring agency, DRRMF from other LGUs by LGU, and other sources by donor.

## Deficiencies in accounting and reporting of LDRRMF and donations

93. Accounting and reporting guidelines on the receipt and utilization of LDRRMF and cash and in-kind donations were not adhered to by 48 LGUs, contrary to COA Circular No. 2014-002; thereby, limiting the transparency and the availability of the necessary information to donors, stakeholders, and the general public on how the DRRM funds were being utilized.

93.1. Items IV and V of COA Circular No. 2014-002 enumerated the accounting and reporting guidelines in the receipt and utilization of DRRM funds. Among these are the following:

The cash donations shall be: (a) acknowledged through the issuance of Official Receipt; (b) deposited with an authorized government depository bank (AGDB) under a separate bank account for DRRMF; and (c) entered in the Cash Receipts Record by the designated Collecting Officer.  
The Accounting Unit shall prepare and maintain a separate SL for "Trust Liabilities-DRRMF" for cash donations received amounting to ₱100,000.00 and above per donor per purpose. At the same time, those below ₱100,000.00 shall be grouped and posted in one SL labeled as "Various Donors" per purpose.  
The Accounting Unit shall prepare and maintain PPE Ledger Cards and Supplies Ledger Cards for all PPE and relief goods procured out of cash donations. For checks and balances, the Property and Supply Unit shall maintain Property Cards and Stock Cards.  
The monthly RSMI shall be prepared by the Property and Supply Unit based on the Requisition Issue Slip (RIS), using the formats in Appendices 59 and 50, respectively, Volume II, MNGAS. The Report shall be submitted to the Accounting Unit for recording in the books of accounts.  
The national and local government agencies with DRRMF allocation from the GAA and/or cash donations received from local and/or foreign sources shall prepare and submit to NDRRMC through the OCD, on the 5<sup>th</sup> day following the end of each month, the Report on the Receipt and Utilization of the DRRMF sourced from GAA (Annex H) and Report on the Receipt and Utilization of Cash Donations (Annex I). The reports shall be furnished to the respective COA Auditor.

93.2. However, various deficiencies were noted in 48 LGUs in the implementation of the accounting and reporting guidelines. Among these are the following:

• Non-submission of RIS and acknowledgment receipts of recipients;  
• Non-maintenance of registries;  
Distribution lists of welfare goods for distribution and food supplies for consumption remained unsupported with the corresponding Summary of Supplies and Materials Issued and the RIS;  
• Report on Cash Donations and Utilization (RCDU) were not prepared and maintained for utilization and monitoring; and

Monthly Report on the Receipt and Utilization of the DRRMF sourced from GAA and Report on the Receipt and Utilization of Cash Donations were not prepared and submitted.

93.3. The deficiencies in the accounting and reporting of the DRRM receipts and utilization limit the transparency and the availability of the necessary information to its donors, stakeholders, and the general public on how the DRRM funds were being utilized.  
93.4. The details are presented in Annex T.  
93.5. The Audit Teams recommended that Management require the concerned officers to strictly adhere to the accounting and reporting guidelines prescribed under COA Circular 2014-002.

Prior Years’ Unexpended Balances of the LDRRMF</td></tr><tr><td colspan="5">I.1 Unexpended QRF and LDRRMF-MOOE not transferred to Special Trust Fund</td></tr><tr><td>Consolidated Report on the Audit of DRRM Funds CY 2023 pg. 85 - 86</td><td>The unexpended balances of the QRF and LDRRMF-MOOE of 284 LGUs, totaling at least ₱2.206 billion, were not transferred to the Special Trust Fund (STF) contrary to Section 5.1.10 of COA Circular No. 2012-002; thus, opens opportunity for the LGU to use it for activities other than those which support DRRM.</td><td>The Auditors recommended that Management transfer the unutilized prior year balances (within five years) of the QRF and LDRRMF-MOOE to the STF pursuant to COA Circular No. 2012-002.</td><td>No information was indicated as to the actions taken by the management. However, out of the 284 LGUs, 146 LGUs have implemented the audit recommendations, and 58 were not implemented. On the other hand, 80 LGUS did not submit report.</td><td>Partially Implemented</td></tr></table>

Annex A

<table><tr><td>Reference</td><td>Observations</td><td>Recommendations</td><td>Management Action</td><td>Status of Implementation</td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>Details are presented in Annex U.</td><td></td></tr><tr><td colspan="5">I.2 Unexpended LDRRMF-Capital Outlay (CO) inappropriately transferred to STF</td></tr><tr><td>Consolidated Report on the Audit of DRRM Funds CY 2023 pg. 86 - 87</td><td>The unexpended/unobligated balance of LDRRMF-CO of 39 LGUs, totaling at least ₱225.631 million was inappropriately transferred to the STF contrary to Section 5.1.11 of COA Circular No. 2012-002.</td><td>The Auditors recommended that the Management transfer the unexpended balance of LDRRMF-CO from the STF to the General Fund and henceforth, ensure that all unexpended/unobligated balance of LDRRMF-CO shall be made continuing in the General Fund books.</td><td>No information was indicated as to the actions taken by the management. However, out of the 39 LGUs, 15 LGUs have implemented the audit recommendations and 5 were not implemented. On the other hand, 19 LGUS did not submit report. Details are presented in Annex U.</td><td>Partially Implemented</td></tr><tr><td colspan="5">I.3 PY unutilized balances of more than five (5) years not reverted to General Fund</td></tr><tr><td>Consolidated Report on the Audit of DRRM Funds CY 2023 pg. 87 - 88</td><td>Unutilized balances of LDRRMF under the STF for CY 2017 and below of 78 LGUs, totaling at least ₱313.890 million, were not transferred to the General Fund contrary to Section 5.1.13 of COA Circular No. 2012-002, hindering the provision of funds to support other PAPs.</td><td>The Auditors recommended that the Management revert to the GF the unexpended balance of LDRRMF beyond the five-year validity period, to be made available for other social services, in conformity with Section 5.1.13 of COA Circular No. 2012-002.</td><td>No information was indicated as to the actions taken by the management. However, out of the 78 LGUs, 41 LGUs have implemented the audit recommendations, and 9 were not implemented. On the other hand, 28 LGUS did not submit report. Details are presented in Annex U.</td><td>Partially Implemented</td></tr></table>

Annex A

<table><tr><td>Reference</td><td>Observations</td><td>Recommendations</td><td>Management Action</td><td>Status of Implementation</td></tr><tr><td colspan="5">I.4 PAPs for the PY&#x27;s unexpended balances not included in the LDRRMFIP</td></tr><tr><td>Consolidated Report on the Audit of DRRM Funds CY 2023 pg. 88 - 89</td><td>LDRRMFIP of 79 LGUs did not include the PAPs charged to the unexpended balances of LDRRMF under Trust Liabilities account, in violation of Sections 4.4 and 5.1.2 of COA Circular No. 2012-002; hence, validity of the transactions could not be ascertained.</td><td>The Auditors recommended that the Management include in their plans the utilization of the unexpended LDRRMF balances from PYs, and specify the list of PAPs to be charged against the unexpended LDRRMF of PYs in its LDRRMFIP prior to its utilization.</td><td>No information was indicated as to the actions taken by the management. However, out of the 79 LGUs, 20 LGUs have implemented the audit recommendations and 28 were not implemented. On the other hand, 31 LGUS did not submit report. Details are presented in Annex U.</td><td>Partially Implemented</td></tr><tr><td colspan="5">J. Submission of Required Reports - Unprepared and/or unsubmitted reports</td></tr><tr><td>Consolidated Report on the Audit of DRRM Funds CY 2023 pg. 89 - 90</td><td>321 LGUs failed to prepare and submit the required: a) Annual LDRRMP; b) Annual LDRRMFIP; c) Registry of Appropriations, Allotments and Obligations (RAAO) for QRF, MOOE and CO; d) Report on Sources and Utilization of DRRMF; and e) Summary/List of Donations Received, Distributed and Balances, causing difficulty in the evaluation of the LDRRMF utilization.</td><td>The Auditors recommended that Management prepare and submit the required financial reports within the prescribed deadline to ensure the availability of information on DRRM funds.</td><td>No information was indicated as to the actions taken by the management. However, out of the 321 LGUs, 67 LGUs have implemented the audit recommendations and 39 were not implemented. On the other hand, 215 LGUS did not submit report. Details are presented in Annex U.</td><td>Partially Implemented</td></tr></table>

Annex A

<table><tr><td>Reference</td><td>Observations</td><td>Recommendations</td><td>Management Action</td><td>Status of Implementation</td></tr><tr><td colspan="5">J.1 Accounting and Financial Disclosures - Non-disclosure in the Notes to Financial Statements (FS)</td></tr><tr><td>Consolidated Report on the Audit of DRRM Funds CY 2023 pg. 90 - 91</td><td>Disclosure requirements prescribed in COA Circular No. 2014-002 related to DRRM Funds were not presented in the Notes to FS of 15 LGUs; thus, the information on the utilization and disbursements of the said funds were not properly disclosed. Moreover, information on the amount and details of the unexpended balance of LDRRMF of 94 LGUs totaling at least ₱158.648 million were not adequately disclosed in the Notes to FS as prescribed under Item 5.1.16 of COA No. 2012-002.</td><td>The Auditors recommended that Management direct the concerned Accountants to fully disclose the receipts of utilization of LDRRMF by providing relevant information in the Notes to FS.</td><td>No information was indicated as to the actions taken by the management. However, out of the 109 LGUs, 44 LGUs have implemented the audit recommendations and 29 were not implemented. On the other hand, 36 LGUS did not submit report. Details are presented in Annex U.</td><td>Partially Implemented</td></tr><tr><td colspan="5">J.2 No separate Subsidiary Ledger (SL) maintained</td></tr><tr><td>Consolidated Report on the Audit of DRRM Funds CY 2023 pg. 91 - 92</td><td>Separate SLs were not maintained by 82 LGUs for transfers of agency&#x27;s unutilized DRRMF to the STF by year of transfer, receipts of NDRRMF by transferring agency, DRRMF from other LGUs, and other sources by donors, in violation of Item 5.1.12 of COA Circular 2012-002.</td><td>The Auditors recommended that Management direct the concerned Accountants to maintain separate SLs for transfers of agency&#x27;s unutilized DRRMF to the STF by year of transfer, receipts of NDRRMF by transferring agency, DRRMF from other LGUs by LGU and other sources by donor.</td><td>No information was indicated as to the actions taken by the management. However, out of the 82 LGUs, 33 LGUs have implemented the audit recommendations and 25 were not implemented. On the other hand, 24 LGUS did not submit report. Details are presented in Annex U.</td><td>Partially Implemented</td></tr></table>

Annex A

<table><tr><td>Reference</td><td>Observations</td><td>Recommendations</td><td>Management Action</td><td>Status of Implementation</td></tr><tr><td colspan="5">J.3 Deficiencies in accounting and reporting of LDRRMF and donations</td></tr><tr><td>Consolidated Report on the Audit of DRRM Funds CY 2023 pg. 92 - 94</td><td>Accounting and reporting guidelines on the receipt and utilization of LDRRMF and cash and in-kind donations were not adhered by 68 LGUs, contrary to Items IV and V of COA Circular No. 2014-002; thereby, limiting the transparency and the availability of the necessary information to donors, stakeholders and the general public on how the DRRM funds were being utilized.</td><td>The Auditors recommended that Management require the concerned officers to strictly adhere with the accounting and reporting guidelines prescribed under COA Circular 2014-002.s</td><td>No information was indicated as to the actions taken by the management. However, out of the 68 LGUs, 21 LGUs have implemented the audit recommendations and 15 were not implemented. On the other hand, 32 LGUS did not submit report. Details are presented in Annex U.</td><td>Partially Implemented</td></tr></table>

Funds of 216 LGUs, amounting to at least ₱963.342 million, were utilized for the purpose other than those allowed for the implementation of disaster programs and activities, in violation of Section 3 of COA Circular No. 2012-002 dated September 11, 2012 and NDRRMC-DBM-DILG Joint Memorandum Circular (JMC) No. 2013- 1 dated March 25, 2013.  
The Auditors recommended that Management strictly adhere to the guidelines set forth under COA Circular No. 2012-002 and NDRRMC-DBM-DILG JMC No. 2013-1, and refrain from charging against the LDRRMF those programs, projects and activities not pertaining to disaster-related activities or not in accordance with the primary purpose for which the fund was established.

Various PPAs incorporated in the LDRRMP of 196 LGUs were not implemented, contrary to Section 21 of RA No. 10121; thus, impeding the efficient use of fund for disaster mitigation, response and rehabilitation to the disadvantage of the public.  
The Auditors recommended that Management ensure timely implementation of all programmed PPAs, in accordance with the respective appropriation ordinance, LDRRMP and AIP, in order to lessen the socio-economic and environmental impacts of disasters in the community and to enhance disaster preparedness and response capabilities of the community.

in four (4) LGUs in violation of Section 12 of RA No. 10121; while the LDRRMOs in 65 LGUs were not fully capacitated/staffed; thus, hindering the capacity of the LGUs to deliver efficiently and effectively the necessary services to ensure the safety of its constituents in the event of disaster, calamity, and other fortuitous events.</td><td colspan="4">The Auditors recommended that the Deficiencies in the LDRRMFIP and Local Sanggunian

<table><tr><td>The utilization of the LDRRMF in 145 LGUs amounting to not less than ₱2.059 billion could not be judiciously evaluated due to the deficiencies in the details of the Local Disaster Risk Reduction Management Fund Investment Plan (LDRRMFIP) and/or lack of Local Sanggunian resolution declaring a State of Calamity.</td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td>The Auditors recommended that LCE direct the DRRMO to submit the LDRRMFIP showing the allocation of LDRRMF for both current and the unexpended funds of prior years, and ensure that the use and release of QRF is supported with Local Sanggunian Resolution and the Declaration of State of Calamity.</td></tr></table>

## PPAs for the PY’s Unexpended Balances Not Included in the LDRRMFIP

LDRRMFIP of 79 LGUs did not include the PPAs charged to the unexpended balances of LDRRMF under Trust Liabilities account, in violation of Sections 4.4 and 5.1.2 of COA Circular No. 2012-002; hence, validity of the transactions could not be ascertained.

The Auditors recommended that the Management include in their plans the utilization of the unexpended LDRRMF balances from PYs, and specify the list of PPAs to be charged against the unexpended LDRRMF of PYs in its LDRRMFIP prior to its utilization.

Non-Disclosure in the Notes to FS

<table><tr><td>Disclosure requirements prescribed in COA Circular No. 2014-002 related to DRRM Funds were not presented in the Notes to FS of 15 LGUs; thus, the information on the utilization and disbursements of the said funds were not properly disclosed. Moreover, information on the amount and details of the unexpended balance of LDRRMF of 94 LGUs totaling at least ₱158.648 million were not adequately disclosed in the Notes to FS as prescribed under Item 5.1.16 of COA No. 2012-002.</td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td>The Auditors recommended that Management direct the concerned Accountants to fully disclose the receipts of utilization of LDRRMF by providing relevant information in the Notes to FS.</td></tr></table>
